Panel content stays mounted across tab changes. - Drawer({id, open, onClose, title}) and DrawerToggle({drawerId, open, onToggle}): opening moves focus in, Esc closes, and focus returns to the toggle. - Modal({open, onClose, title, initialFocusRef?, closeOnBackdrop?, hideCloseButton?}): focus trap, Esc to close, and focus returns to where it was. Keep it mounted and toggle open. - Tooltip({content, placement, delayMs}) wrapping one focusable element: shows on hover and focus, Esc dismisses it, and it renders in a portal so scroll areas don't clip it. - Extras: Badge({tone: 'neutral'|'warn'}), and DotGlyph / ReplicaGlyph legend marks drawn from the same encodings as the canvas. - Modal, drawer and tab transitions use motion/react and have zero duration under prefers-reduced-motion. A CSS rule also zeroes CSS transitions.

## Decisions to confirm 1. Tabs default to manual activation: arrow keys move focus, Enter or Space selects. Switching a scenario tab resets the run, so arrowing past a tab shouldn't reload it. 2. The tabs and toolbar stay at the top and the timeline stays at the bottom; only the canvas and charts scroll. 3. The drawer is an inline panel that slides open under the toolbar (it goes inside the toolbar slot), not an overlay. 4. The small-window notice is a banner, not a blocking screen. The app stays mounted and the page scrolls. 5. Fork, incident and playhead markers use neutral ink plus a glyph, not their own colors. Any orange dark enough for a thin line on white looks the same as vermillion (p99) under deuteranopia (ΔE 3.9).

# Engine core: event queue and day runner

WP E2 (00-build §5). Every engine module (E5 replica, E6 load and client, E7 router, E8 failure, E9 metrics) plugs into this. E11 builds the Engine from it.

The runner simulates one independent day (K21) from a standard morning state. It advances by jumping from event to event (02 §5), checkpoints with `structuredClone`, and restores with different patches to fork (04 §3).

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `types.ts` | `DayState`, `Ctx`, `EngineModule`, `defineModule`, `CoreDayRun`, `DayRunner` |
| `runner.ts` | `createDayRunner(modules)`: `createDayRun` / `restoreDayRun`; `advanceInSteps` |
| `run.ts` | The advance loop, bucket hooks, patch application, checkpoints |
| `queue.ts` | Binary heap of events as plain data, with O(1) cancellation |
| `ids.ts` | `PRIORITY` bands, `KIND_RANGES`, shared `TOPIC`s |
| `level.ts` | Time-weighted levels (KV usage, queue lengths) |
| `patches.ts` | Pre-day and in-day patch split (K21) |
| `chunk.ts` | `emptyChunk`, the stub producer until E9 lands |
| `plain.ts` | `assertPlainData`, `digestState`, `canonicalJson` |
| `toy-model.test.ts` | A complete toy model: the best runnable example |

Import from `../core/index.ts`.

## The model in one paragraph

State is one plain-data object: `state.core` plus one **slice** per module (`state.replica`, `state.load`, ...). A **module** owns a slice, a set of **event kinds** with handlers `(state, ev, ctx) => void`, and optional hooks. Handlers read any slice but write only their own, except through the other module's exported functions. Events carry two numbers, `a` and `b`. `ctx.schedule` returns a **handle**, and `ctx.cancel(handle)` invalidates the event in O(1). A cancelled event never reaches its handler. A **notice** (`ctx.notify(topic, a, b)`) calls every subscriber synchronously; it is for upward and fan-out calls (a first token, a request ending). Tunable parameters in effect live in `state.core.params`; patches change them.

## Worked example

This module is illustrative, not E7's design: a router-signal sampler that copies each replica's outstanding count every `signalRefreshMs`. It shows a slice with its augmentation, init, a self-rescheduling event, reading another slice, `onParams`, a notice, a level flushed at bucket ends, and invariants. `readme-example.test.ts` compiles and runs it, with test slice names.

```ts
// src/engine/router/signals.ts
import {
  NO_EVENT, PRIORITY, TOPIC, createLevel, defineModule, setLevel, takeLevelMean,
  type Ctx, type DayState, type Level,
} from '../core/index.ts';
import { REPLICA_STATE } from '../results.ts';

export interface SignalSlice {
  /** Outstanding count per replica as the router last saw it (stale by design, 02 §7). */
  seen: Float64Array;
  ready: Uint8Array;
  /** Handle of the next refresh. Handles need 53 bits: Float64Array or number[], never Int32Array. */
  refreshEv: number;
  staleness: Level;
}

// 1. Add the slice to DayState. Augment the declaring file, not the index barrel.
declare module '../core/types.ts' {
  interface DayState {
    signals: SignalSlice;
  }
}

// 2. Event kinds come from this module's KIND_RANGES range (router: 200-299).
export const EV_REFRESH = 200;

function refresh(state: DayState, ctx: Ctx): void {
  const s = state.signals;
  const replicas = state.replica; // read another module's slice; don't write it
  for (let r = 0; r < s.seen.length; r++) s.seen[r] = replicas.outstanding[r]!;
  setLevel(s.staleness, ctx.nowMs, 0);
  // Payloads are numbers. Returns NO_EVENT if the time falls after the day's end.
  s.refreshEv = ctx.schedule(ctx.nowMs + state.core.params.signalRefreshMs, EV_REFRESH);
}

export const signalsModule = defineModule({
  name: 'signals',
  // 3. The standard morning state. params already include 'set' patches dated before the day.
  init(_state, ctx) {
    const n = ctx.input.config.replicas;
    const refreshEv = ctx.schedule(ctx.dayStartMs, EV_REFRESH);
    return {
      seen: new Float64Array(n),
      ready: new Uint8Array(n).fill(1),
      refreshEv,
      staleness: createLevel(ctx.nowMs),
    };
  },
  events: [{ kind: EV_REFRESH, name: 'signals.refresh', priority: PRIORITY.router, handle: (state, _ev, ctx) => refresh(state, ctx) }],
  // 4. A lasting parameter changed at ctx.nowMs: move the pending refresh.

85add0d Integrator: E4 merged; wave 2 starting
  // 4. A lasting parameter changed at ctx.nowMs: move the pending refresh.
  onParams(state, changes, ctx) {
    if (changes.signalRefreshMs === undefined) return;
    const s = state.signals;
    s.refreshEv = ctx.reschedule(s.refreshEv, ctx.nowMs + changes.signalRefreshMs, EV_REFRESH);
  },
  // 5. Upward notices: E8 announces replica state changes; nobody imports anybody.
  notices: [
    {
      topic: TOPIC.replicaState,
      handle(state, n) {
        state.signals.ready[n.a] = n.b === REPLICA_STATE.ready ? 1 : 0;
      },
    },
  ],
  // 6. Levels integrate lazily; flush them at bucket ends (E9 will own this in practice).
  onBucketEnd(state, boundaryMs) {
    takeLevelMean(state.signals.staleness, boundaryMs);
  },
  assertInvariants(state, ctx) {
    if (!ctx.isPending(state.signals.refreshEv) && state.signals.refreshEv !== NO_EVENT) {
      throw new Error('signals: lost the refresh event');
    }
  },
});
```

const runner = createDayRunner([loadModule, routerModule, replicaModule, failureModule, metricsModule]);

## API

```ts
createDayRunner(modules: readonly EngineModule[], options?: RunnerOptions): DayRunner
interface DayRunner {
  createDayRun(input: DayRunInput): CoreDayRun;
  restoreDayRun(input: DayRunInput, checkpoint: DayCheckpoint): CoreDayRun;
}
interface CoreDayRun extends DayRun {           // DayRun from api.ts: day, nowMs, done, advance, checkpoint
  readonly state: DayState;                     // live; E11 reads module outputs (e.g. rollup) here
  readonly dayEndMs: SimMs;
  assertInvariants(): void;                     // core + every module + plain-data walk
}
interface RunnerOptions {
  trace?: (ev: Readonly<EventView>, state: DayState) => void;  // before each dispatch; patches have kind PATCH_KIND (0)
  assertEveryEvent?: boolean;                   // core + module invariants after every event (tests)
}
advanceInSteps(run: DayRun, untilMs: SimMs, stepMs: number): ResultChunk[]

interface EngineModule<N extends SliceName> {
  readonly name: N;                                         // slice key; 'core' is reserved
  init(state: DayState, ctx: Ctx): DayState[N];             // may schedule; later slices don't exist yet
  readonly events?: readonly EventSpec[];                   // { kind, name, priority, handle }
  readonly notices?: readonly NoticeSpec[];                 // { topic, handle }
  onParams?(state, changes: Partial<TunableParams>, ctx): void;   // after state.core.params updated
  onInjected?(state, event: InjectedEvent, ctx): void;      // every module sees every injected event
  onBucketEnd?(state, boundaryMs: SimMs, ctx): void;        // ctx.nowMs === boundaryMs
  produceChunk?(state, span: ChunkSpan, ctx): ResultChunk;  // at most one module (E9)
  assertInvariants?(state, ctx): void;
}
type EventHandler  = (state: DayState, ev: Readonly<EventView>,  ctx: Ctx) => void;  // ev: { atMs, kind, a, b, handle }
type NoticeHandler = (state: DayState, n: Readonly<NoticeView>, ctx: Ctx) => void;   // n: { topic, a, b }

interface Ctx {
  readonly nowMs: SimMs;
  readonly input: DayRunInput;         // config, calibration, day, detail, trackedAnalyst
  readonly dayStartMs: SimMs;
  readonly dayEndMs: SimMs;
  schedule(atMs: SimMs, kind: number, a?: number, b?: number): EventHandle;
  cancel(handle: EventHandle): boolean;
  reschedule(handle: EventHandle, atMs: SimMs, kind: number, a?: number, b?: number): EventHandle;
  isPending(handle: EventHandle): boolean;
  notify(topic: number, a?: number, b?: number): void;
}
```

## Rules

### Time and the boundary rule

`advance(untilMs)` with `end = min(untilMs, dayEndMs)`:

1. Take the earliest of the next pending patch and the next queued event. A patch wins a tie. Stop once that time is `>= end`. **Events and patches exactly at `end` stay pending.**
2. Before anything at time `t` runs, close every bucket boundary `b <= t` in order: `onBucketEnd(state, b)` runs with `nowMs = b`, after every event before `b` and before any event at `b`. An event at exactly a boundary lands in the new bucket.
3. Set `nowMs = t`, then apply the patch or dispatch the event.
4. Close boundaries `<= end`, set `nowMs = end`, and build the chunk for `[fromMs, end)`.

So after `advance(T)`, every event before `T` has run and none at or after `T` has. That is the checkpoint invariant. The day is `[dayStartMs, dayStartMs + DAY_MS)`. `schedule` at or after `dayEndMs` returns `NO_EVENT` and queues nothing, so no event ever runs at the day end. `advance` past the end clamps, and `done` becomes true. Moving backwards throws. Splitting one advance into many runs the same handlers in the same order on the same state; the toy model proves it with a full-state digest.

`schedule` throws for `atMs < nowMs` or NaN. Event-jumping math can land a hair before now through float error, so clamp: `ctx.nowMs + Math.max(0, remaining) * rate`. `atMs === nowMs` is allowed; the event runs later in the same instant.

### Ordering at equal times

Order is `(atMs, priority, push sequence)`: a strict total order, independent of heap layout. Use the `PRIORITY` bands. A module may add 0–9 to order its own kinds.

| Band | Value | Kinds |
|---|---|---|
| (patches) | first | Applied by the core before any event at their instant |
| `infra` | 10 | E8: crash, mark-down, load phases, Ready |
| `engine` | 20 | E5: step-span ends (first token, finish, prefill chunk, KV exhaustion) |
| `client` | 30 | E6: timeouts. After `engine`, so a first token exactly at the deadline counts (K8) |
| `router` | 40 | E7: signal refresh, dispatch after router overhead |
| `arrival` | 50 | E6: session start, next turn, retry |
| `late` | 90 | Anything that must see the instant settled |

### Kinds and topics

Kinds and topics are integers in `[1, 1024)`. Each module takes them from its own range, and the runner rejects duplicate kinds when it is built.

| Range | Owner |
|---|---|
| 1–99 | core (shared topics below) |
| 100–199 | load and client (E6) |
| 200–299 | router (E7) |
| 300–399 | replica (E5) |
| 400–499 | failure (E8) |
| 500–599 | metrics (E9) |

// Shared numbering for event kinds, priorities, and notice topics (see README.md).
// Numbers, not strings, so the queue stores them in typed arrays and dispatch is an array lookup.

/** Event kinds and topics are integers in [1, MAX_KIND). 0 is reserved (PATCH_KIND in traces). */
export const MAX_KIND = 1024;
export const MAX_TOPIC = 1024;
/** Kind reported to RunnerOptions.trace for a patch applied by the core. */
export const PATCH_KIND = 0;

/**
 * Each module picks its event kinds and topics from its own range, so parallel WPs never collide.
 * The runner rejects duplicate kinds when it is built.
 */
export const KIND_RANGES = {
  core: [1, 99],
  load: [100, 199], // E6 load generator and client
  router: [200, 299], // E7
  replica: [300, 399], // E5 replica scheduler
  failure: [400, 499], // E8
  metrics: [500, 599], // E9
  spare: [600, 899],
  test: [900, 1023], // toy models in tests
} as const satisfies Record<string, readonly [number, number]>;

/**
 * Priority bands: at equal atMs, lower priority fires first; within a kind, push order decides.
 * A module may use band + 0..9 to order its own kinds. Patches are applied before every event at
 * their instant (they are not queued; see README "Patches").
 *
 * Rationale for the order, all at the same instant:
 * - infra: a crash or Ready changes which replicas exist before any request event sees them.
 * - engine: completions and first tokens land before timeouts, so a first token exactly at the
 *   deadline counts (K8: "no token has arrived by then"), and freed capacity is visible to arrivals.
 * - client: timeouts, then router signal refresh, then new arrivals and retries.
 * - late: observation that must see the instant settled.
 */
export const PRIORITY = {
  infra: 10, // E8: crash, mark-down, load phases, Ready
  engine: 20, // E5: step-span ends (first token, finish, prefill chunk, KV exhaustion)
  client: 30, // E6: client timeouts
  router: 40, // E7: signal refresh, dispatch after router overhead
  arrival: 50, // E6: session start, next turn, retry
  late: 90,
} as const;

/**
 * Shared request-lifecycle topics (02 §3). Subscribers run synchronously, in module order, when a
 * module calls ctx.notify(topic, a, b). Payloads are two numbers.
 */
export const TOPIC = {
  /** A request produced its first output token. a = RequestId, b = ReplicaId. Emitted by E5. */
  firstToken: 1,
  /**
   * A request reached its outcome; emitted once per request by the module that ends it.
   * a = RequestId, b = OUTCOME code from results.ts (finished, rejected, timedOut, failed).
   */
  requestEnded: 2,
  /** A replica changed state. a = ReplicaId, b = REPLICA_STATE code from results.ts. Emitted by E8. */
  replicaState: 3,
} as const;

// Time-weighted levels (KV usage, running and waiting counts) without a per-event hook.
//
// A Level integrates lazily: each change adds value × elapsed to `area`, so the cost is per change,
// not per event per module. At a bucket boundary (onBucketEnd), takeLevelMean closes the interval
// and returns the time-weighted mean since the previous take. Plain data; keep Levels in a slice.

import type { SimMs } from '../time.ts';

export interface Level {
  value: number;
  /** Time up to which `area` is integrated. */
  sinceMs: SimMs;
  /** ∫ value dt since the last take, in value × ms. */
  area: number;
  /** Largest value held since the last take. */
  max: number;
  /** Start of the interval the next take averages over. */
  openedMs: SimMs;
}

export function createLevel(atMs: SimMs, value = 0): Level {
  return { value, sinceMs: atMs, area: 0, max: value, openedMs: atMs };
}

/** The level becomes `value` at atMs (atMs must not go backwards). */
export function setLevel(level: Level, atMs: SimMs, value: number): void {
  if (atMs < level.sinceMs) throw new RangeError(`Level set at ${atMs} before ${level.sinceMs}`);
  level.area += level.value * (atMs - level.sinceMs);
  level.sinceMs = atMs;
  level.value = value;
  if (value > level.max) level.max = value;
}

export function addLevel(level: Level, atMs: SimMs, delta: number): void {
  setLevel(level, atMs, level.value + delta);
}

/**
 * Time-weighted mean over [openedMs, atMs), then starts a new interval at atMs. Read `max` first if
 * you need it; the take resets it to the current value. Returns the current value for an empty
 * interval.
 */
export function takeLevelMean(level: Level, atMs: SimMs): number {
  setLevel(level, atMs, level.value);
  const span = atMs - level.openedMs;
  const mean = span > 0 ? level.area / span : level.value;
  level.area = 0;
  level.max = level.value;
  level.openedMs = atMs;
  return mean;
}

// The shared request table (docs/00-build.md §4). Integrator-owned contract for E5, E6, E7, E9.
//
// One slot per live request, struct-of-arrays in typed arrays, so it checkpoints cheaply and only
// holds requests in flight (thousands, not the day's hundreds of thousands). Topics pass the slot
// (RequestSlot); results use the stable day-local `id`.
//
// Field ownership (write only your own fields; read anything):
// - E6 load/client, at creation: id is assigned by allocRequest; session, analyst, turn, attempt,
//   kind, arriveMs, promptTokens, systemPromptTokens, outputTarget, prevReplica. It then sets
//   state = atRouter and notifies requestArrived.
// - E7 router: dispatchMs, replica.
// - E5 replica: firstTokenMs, cachedTokens, outputDone, preemptions.
// - Whoever ends a request (E7 on reject or cancel before dispatch, E5 otherwise) sets endMs,
//   outcome, and state, then notifies requestEnded.
// - Every module that changes `state` notifies requestState.
//
// Slot reuse: after requestEnded, the shared module queues the slot, and allocRequest frees queued
// slots before it allocates. So never allocate inside a notice handler: schedule an event instead
// (a retry with zero backoff is an event at nowMs). That guarantees every requestEnded subscriber
// has read the slot before it is reused.
//
// Growth replaces the arrays: never hold a field array across a call to allocRequest.

import { REQUEST_STATE } from '../results.ts';

/** Index into the request table; valid from allocRequest until the slot is reused. */
export type RequestSlot = number;

export const REQUEST_KIND = { turn: 0, extra: 1 } as const;

/** outcome before the request ends. OUTCOME codes (results.ts) are nonzero. */
export const OUTCOME_PENDING = 0;

export interface RequestTable {
  capacity: number;
  /** Next day-local request id. */
  nextId: number;
  /** Slots in use. */
  liveCount: number;
  freeSlots: number[];
  /** Ended slots waiting to be freed at the next allocRequest. */
  pendingFree: number[];

  live: Uint8Array;
  id: Uint32Array;
  session: Uint32Array;
  analyst: Uint32Array;
  /** 1-based turn within the session; 1 for extra requests. */
  turn: Uint16Array;
  /** 0 for the first attempt. */
  attempt: Uint8Array;
  kind: Uint8Array;
  /** REQUEST_STATE. */
  state: Uint8Array;
  /** -1 until dispatched. */
  replica: Int8Array;
  /** Replica that served the session's previous completed turn; -1 if none. */
  prevReplica: Int8Array;
  arriveMs: Float64Array;
  dispatchMs: Float64Array;
  firstTokenMs: Float64Array;
  endMs: Float64Array;
  /** Whole prompt: system prompt + conversation history + new message. */
  promptTokens: Uint32Array;
  /** System prompt length in effect when the request was created (for KV block identity, E4). */
  systemPromptTokens: Uint32Array;
  /** Tokens the simulator will generate (known to the simulator, not the router, 02 §1). */
  outputTarget: Uint32Array;
  /** Tokens generated when the request ended. */
  outputDone: Uint32Array;
  /** Prompt tokens served from the prefix cache at the (last) admission. */
  cachedTokens: Uint32Array;
  preemptions: Uint16Array;
  /** OUTCOME code, or OUTCOME_PENDING. */
  outcome: Uint8Array;
}

const U8 = ['live', 'attempt', 'kind', 'state', 'outcome'] as const;
const I8 = ['replica', 'prevReplica'] as const;
const U16 = ['turn', 'preemptions'] as const;
const U32 = [
  'id',
  'session',
  'analyst',
  'promptTokens',
  'systemPromptTokens',
  'outputTarget',
  'outputDone',
  'cachedTokens',
] as const;
const F64 = ['arriveMs', 'dispatchMs', 'firstTokenMs', 'endMs'] as const;

export function createRequestTable(capacity = 1024): RequestTable {
  const t = {
    capacity,
    nextId: 0,
    liveCount: 0,
    freeSlots: [] as number[],
    pendingFree: [] as number[],
  } as RequestTable;
  for (const f of U8) t[f] = new Uint8Array(capacity);
  for (const f of I8) t[f] = new Int8Array(capacity).fill(-1);
  for (const f of U16) t[f] = new Uint16Array(capacity);
  for (const f of U32) t[f] = new Uint32Array(capacity);
  for (const f of F64) t[f] = new Float64Array(capacity).fill(NaN);
  for (let i = capacity - 1; i >= 0; i--) t.freeSlots.push(i);
  return t;
}

function grow(t: RequestTable): void {
  const old = t.capacity;
  const cap = old * 2;
  const copy = <A extends Uint8Array | Int8Array | Uint16Array | Uint32Array | Float64Array>(
    a: A,
    make: (n: number) => A,
    fill?: number,
  ): A => {
    const b = make(cap);
    if (fill !== undefined) b.fill(fill);
    b.set(a);
    return b;
  };
  for (const f of U8) t[f] = copy(t[f], (n) => new Uint8Array(n));
  for (const f of I8) t[f] = copy(t[f], (n) => new Int8Array(n), -1);
  for (const f of U16) t[f] = copy(t[f], (n) => new Uint16Array(n));
  for (const f of U32) t[f] = copy(t[f], (n) => new Uint32Array(n));
  for (const f of F64) t[f] = copy(t[f], (n) => new Float64Array(n), NaN);
  for (let i = cap - 1; i >= old; i--) t.freeSlots.push(i);
  t.capacity = cap;
}

function clearSlot(t: RequestTable, s: RequestSlot): void {
  for (const f of U8) t[f][s] = 0;
  for (const f of I8) t[f][s] = -1;
  for (const f of U16) t[f][s] = 0;
  for (const f of U32) t[f][s] = 0;
  for (const f of F64) t[f][s] = NaN;
}

/**
 * A fresh slot with a new day-local id, state atRouter, everything else cleared. Frees slots ended
 * earlier first. Never call from a notice handler (see the file header).
 */
export function allocRequest(t: RequestTable): RequestSlot {
  while (t.pendingFree.length > 0) {
    const s = t.pendingFree.pop()!;
    clearSlot(t, s);
    t.freeSlots.push(s);
    t.liveCount--;
  }
  if (t.freeSlots.length === 0) grow(t);
  const s = t.freeSlots.pop()!;
  t.live[s] = 1;
  t.id[s] = t.nextId++;
  t.state[s] = REQUEST_STATE.atRouter;
  t.liveCount++;
  return s;
}

/** Queues an ended slot for reuse. Called by the shared module's requestEnded subscriber. */
export function releaseRequest(t: RequestTable, s: RequestSlot): void {
  if (t.live[s] !== 1) throw new Error(`Request slot ${s} released twice or never allocated`);
  t.live[s] = 2;
  t.pendingFree.push(s);
}

export function isLive(t: RequestTable, s: RequestSlot): boolean {
  return t.live[s] === 1;
}

export function assertRequestTable(t: RequestTable): void {
  let live = 0;
  for (let s = 0; s < t.capacity; s++) if (t.live[s] !== 0) live++;
  if (live !== t.liveCount) throw new Error(`Request table: liveCount ${t.liveCount}, found ${live}`);
  if (live + t.freeSlots.length !== t.capacity) {
    throw new Error('Request table: live + free slots != capacity');
  }
}
EOF

// Shared meters (docs/00-build.md §4): counters and levels that E9 turns into ScalarBlock metrics.
// E9 observes most request counts itself through topics; meters hold what only the owner can see.
//
// Ownership:
// - E5 replica: every ReplicaMeters counter, plus the kvUsed, running, and waiting levels. At each
//   bucket end, E5's onBucketEnd pro-rates any step in progress into the counters, so E9 (later in
//   module order) reads values exact to the boundary.
// - E7 router: the outstanding levels.
// - E6 client: fleet.abandonedSessions.
// Counters are cumulative since the day's start; E9 diffs them at bucket ends and takes level means.

import { createLevel, type Level } from '../core/level.ts';
import type { SimMs } from '../time.ts';

export interface ReplicaMeters {
  prefillTokens: Float64Array;
  recomputedPrefillTokens: Float64Array;
  decodeTokens: Float64Array;
  /** nvidia-smi-style busy time: whole steps including t_o (E3). */
  busyMs: Float64Array;
  flops: Float64Array;
  preemptions: Float64Array;
  evictedBlocks: Float64Array;
  prefixQueryTokens: Float64Array;
  prefixHitTokens: Float64Array;
  /** Prefix lookups for turns >= 2 only (tab 4). */
  returningQueryTokens: Float64Array;
  returningHitTokens: Float64Array;
  /** Levels, one per replica. kvUsed is the referenced-block fraction (E4 kvUsedFrac). */
  kvUsed: Level[];
  running: Level[];
  waiting: Level[];
  /** Router-tracked dispatched-and-not-ended count (E7). */
  outstanding: Level[];
}

export interface FleetMeters {
  abandonedSessions: number;
}

export interface Meters {
  replica: ReplicaMeters;
  fleet: FleetMeters;
}

export const REPLICA_COUNTERS = [
  'prefillTokens',
  'recomputedPrefillTokens',
  'decodeTokens',
  'busyMs',
  'flops',
  'preemptions',
  'evictedBlocks',
  'prefixQueryTokens',
  'prefixHitTokens',
  'returningQueryTokens',
  'returningHitTokens',
] as const satisfies readonly (keyof ReplicaMeters)[];

export function createMeters(replicas: number, atMs: SimMs): Meters {
  const levels = () => Array.from({ length: replicas }, () => createLevel(atMs));
  const replica = { kvUsed: levels(), running: levels(), waiting: levels(), outstanding: levels() } as ReplicaMeters;
  for (const c of REPLICA_COUNTERS) replica[c] = new Float64Array(replicas);
  return { replica, fleet: { abandonedSessions: 0 } };
}

cat > src/engine/shared/module.ts <<'EOF'
// The shared slice: the request table and meters. First in module order, so every other module's
// init can see it. It frees request slots after requestEnded (see requests.ts).

import { defineModule } from '../core/types.ts';
import { TOPIC } from '../core/ids.ts';
import { createMeters, type Meters } from './meters.ts';
import { assertRequestTable, createRequestTable, releaseRequest, type RequestTable } from './requests.ts';

export interface SharedSlice {
  requests: RequestTable;
  meters: Meters;
}

declare module '../core/types.ts' {
  interface DayState {
    shared: SharedSlice;
  }
}

export const sharedModule = defineModule({
  name: 'shared',
  init(_state, ctx) {
    return {
      requests: createRequestTable(),
      meters: createMeters(ctx.input.config.replicas, ctx.nowMs),
    };
  },
  notices: [
    {
      topic: TOPIC.requestEnded,
      handle(state, n) {
        releaseRequest(state.shared.requests, n.a);
      },
    },
  ],
  assertInvariants(state) {
    assertRequestTable(state.shared.requests);
  },
});
EOF

cat > src/engine/shared/requests.test.ts <<'EOF'
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { OUTCOME, REQUEST_STATE } from '../results.ts';
import {
  allocRequest,
  assertRequestTable,
  createRequestTable,
  isLive,
  releaseRequest,
} from './requests.ts';

describe('request table', () => {
  it('assigns increasing ids and starts requests at the router', () => {
    const t = createRequestTable(2);
    const a = allocRequest(t);
    const b = allocRequest(t);
    expect([t.id[a], t.id[b]]).toEqual([0, 1]);
    expect(t.state[a]).toBe(REQUEST_STATE.atRouter);
    expect(t.replica[a]).toBe(-1);
    expect(t.arriveMs[a]).toBeNaN();
  });

  it('grows and keeps field values', () => {
    const t = createRequestTable(2);
    const slots = Array.from({ length: 5 }, () => allocRequest(t));
    t.promptTokens[slots[1]!] = 1234;
    allocRequest(t);
    expect(t.capacity).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(6);
    expect(t.promptTokens[slots[1]!]).toBe(1234);
    assertRequestTable(t);
  });

  it('reuses a released slot only at the next allocation, cleared', () => {
    const t = createRequestTable(1);
    const a = allocRequest(t);
    t.outcome[a] = OUTCOME.finished;
    releaseRequest(t, a);
    expect(t.outcome[a]).toBe(OUTCOME.finished); // still readable by later subscribers
    expect(isLive(t, a)).toBe(false);
    const b = allocRequest(t);
    expect(b).toBe(a);
    expect(t.outcome[b]).toBe(0);
    expect(t.id[b]).toBe(1);
    assertRequestTable(t);
  });

  it('survives structuredClone', () => {
    const t = createRequestTable(4);
    allocRequest(t);
    const c = structuredClone(t);
    expect(allocRequest(c)).toBe(allocRequest(t));
  });

  it('rejects a double release', () => {
    const t = createRequestTable(1);
    const a = allocRequest(t);
    releaseRequest(t, a);
    expect(() => releaseRequest(t, a)).toThrow();
  });

0dc0e38 Integrator: engine module contract (shared request table, meters, lifecycle topics)
// KV block manager and prefix cache (WP E4; 02 §7 rules 3 and 5). One KvPool per replica.
//
// How a request uses it (E5 scheduler, E10 oracle). `r.blocks` is the request's block table
// (sequence block i at index i), `r.held` how many blocks it holds, `r.registered` how many leading
// blocks have been through registration, `L` the tokens it must hold (prompt, plus output so far
// after a preemption), `S` its system prompt length, `s` its session.
//
//   Admit (waiting → running):
//     hits = longestCachedPrefix(pool, s, S, L, r.blocks, 0)          // cached = hits × blockSize
//     if (!canAcquire(pool, r.blocks, 0, hits, blocksForTokens(pool, L) - hits)) keep waiting
//     acquireBlocks(pool, r.blocks, 0, hits, blocksForTokens(pool, cached + chunk) - hits)
//     r.held = hits + that; r.registered = hits
//     Do the lookup and the acquire with no allocation or release between them.
//   Grow (next chunk, or a decode step that crosses a block boundary):
//     allocateBlocks(pool, need, r.blocks, r.held) || preempt (02 §7 rule 4)
//   After tokens are computed (their KV exists), and before any release:
//     r.registered = registerFullBlocks(pool, s, S, r.blocks, 0, r.registered, computedTokens)
//     A finished request's last output token was never fed back, so its KV is P + O − 1 tokens.
//     Registration needs the blocks still held; unregistered full blocks are lost on release.
//   Finish, abort, or preempt (register first, so the blocks stay cached):
//     releaseBlocks(pool, r.blocks, 0, r.held); r.held = 0; r.registered = 0
//   After any acquireBlocks / allocateBlocks:
//     pool.evictedKeys[0 .. pool.evictedCount) are the evicted contents (Evict events);
//     keySession(key) is the session that lost that block (-1 for the system prompt).
//   Crash: resetKvPool(pool) and drop every block table. Morning (K21): createMorningKvPool.

export { cachedBlock, cachedKeyCount } from './content.ts';
export {
  KEY_BLOCK_SPAN,
  MAX_KEY_SESSION,
  NO_KEY,
  isSystemKey,
  keyBlockIndex,
  keySession,
  sequenceBlockKey,
  sessionBlockKey,
  systemBlockKey,
} from './keys.ts';
export {
  type KvPool,

Don't wait for them; stub them in tests.\n\n## WP E5 · Replica scheduler, mirroring vLLM V1, with event-jumping (owns `src/engine/replica/`)\nA module named `replica`, with event kinds 300–399 at priority band `engine`, exporting `replicaModule`. It is the heart of the simulator, so correctness comes first.\n\n**Slice.** Per replica:\n- a KvPool: `createMorningKvPool` at the day's start (K21), using `calibration.engine` with `config.engineOverrides` applied and the system prompt length from `state.core.params`;\n- a waiting queue (FIFO; preempted requests go to the front) and a running set with admission order;\n- per-request engine state (block table, held and registered counts, computed prompt tokens, generated tokens), keyed by RequestSlot;\n- running aggregates for E3's StepDesc;\n- the current step or span and its event handle;\n- the replica state.\n\n**Inputs (notices):**\n- `requestDispatched` (a = slot, b = replica): enqueue. If the replica is crashed, down, or loading, the connection resets: end the request at once with OUTCOME.failed.\n- `requestCancelled` (a = slot): if you hold the request, remove it, register then release its blocks, set endMs, outcome timedOut and state, then notify requestState and requestEnded.\n- `replicaState` (a = replica, b = state):\n  - crashed: fail every request on that replica (queued and running) with OUTCOME.failed, wipe the pool (`resetKvPool`), and stop stepping;\n  - loadingWeights / initializingEngine / down: idle;\n  - ready after a crash: rejoin with an empty pool (`createKvPool`, not the morning state). E8 emits these; your tests emit them directly.\n\n**Scheduling (02 §7, vLLM V1):**\n1. Admission: a waiting request is admitted when blocks for its whole uncached prompt fit (`canAcquire`, which matches vLLM's can_fit_full_sequence). Prefix hits reduce the blocks needed (`longestCachedPrefix`, capped at prompt − 1); allocate the first chunk.\n2. Step composition: running decodes first, one token each. Prefill chunks then fill the remaining `max_num_batched_tokens` within `max_num_seqs`.\n3. Block growth at block boundaries.\n4. Preemption: when a block can't be allocated, preempt the most recently admitted running request (register, release, back to the front of waiting, recompute later). It may recover part of its prefill through the prefix cache.\n\nAt the end of the step that completes a request's prefill, it emits its first token: set firstTokenMs and cachedTokens, then notify `firstToken` (a = slot, b = replica). A request finishes when its generated tokens reach outputTarget: register, release, set outputDone, endMs, outcome, and state, then notify requestState and requestEnded. Notify `requestState` on every state change (waiting, prefill, decode, preempted, and terminal states). Step durations come from E3 `stepTime`.\n\n**Event-jumping (02 §5).**\n- While a replica runs decode-only with nothing admissible waiting, jump over k steps with E3's `decodeSpan` in one event. k runs to the earliest of the next finish or the step where a block allocation would fail (a preemption).\n- Allocations at boundaries inside the span happen in order, as evictions from the LRU, and are recorded; they change cache contents, not the batch.\n- When something external arrives mid-span (an enqueue, a cancel, a crash), truncate: count the steps completed by `ctx.nowMs` (`decodeSpanStepsWithin`), apply them, and let the step in progress finish atomically (a new arrival waits for the current step to end). Then recompute the plan.\n- Prefill steps are single steps.\n- Clamp every scheduled time to at least `nowMs` (see the core README's float note).\n- Also expose a debug option to disable jumping (one event per step). E10's differential oracle and your own tests use it as a second implementation to cross-check.\n\n**Meters** (`src/engine/shared/meters.ts`; you own every ReplicaMeters counter and the kvUsed, running, and waiting levels):\n- Keep counters cumulative.\n- Count prefix query and hit tokens at each admission, and the returning* counters for turn ≥ 2.\n- Count recomputed prefill tokens after a preemption, and evictions.\n- Busy time is the whole step, including t_o (E3's nvidia-smi definition).\n- In `onBucketEnd`, pro-rate the step or span in progress linearly up to the boundary into the counters, so E9 (later in module order) reads exact values.\n\n**Invariants (`assertInvariants`):** every live request you hold is in exactly one queue with a matching state; block refcounts agree with your block tables (`assertKvInvariants` with heldCounts); the aggregates match a recomputation; the event handle is pending iff there is work.\n\n## Tests\nBuild a small harness: `createDayRunner([sharedModule, a test driver module, replicaModule])`. The driver allocates requests from its own events (never inside a notice) and notifies requestDispatched, plus requestCancelled and replicaState when told to.\n\nCover:\n- each scheduling rule;\n- first-token and finish times against E3's reference helpers for batch 1;\n- a KV-exhaustion scenario with preemption and recompute;\n- prefix-cache reuse across turns of one session, and cold after eviction;\n- cancel in every state; crash and rejoin;\n- jumping versus no-jumping giving identical per-request times (≤ 1e-6 relative) and identical preemption and cached-token counts over many seeded random workloads;\n- checkpoint and restore mid-span equal to an uninterrupted run;\n- `assertEveryEvent` on in tests;\n- a throughput number (events/s, and simulated seconds per wall second) for one replica at knee-level load. It is last in module order and the only module that implements `produceChunk`. Export `metricsModule` and a function E11 calls at the end of the day to get RollupRow[].\n\n**Keep state small.** Checkpoints clone the whole day state, so the slice holds only what isn't emitted yet: the open buckets' accumulators and completed buckets and records since the last `produceChunk`. `produceChunk` moves them out into the ResultChunk (typed arrays, with the alloc helpers) and clears them. Also keep the previous values of the cumulative meters for diffing, and small per-day totals for the rollup.\n\n**Scalars, per series (0 = fleet, r + 1 = replica r), one bucket per `bucketMs`:**\n- **From topics:**\n  - dispatched: requestDispatched, per replica;\n  - finished, timedOut, failed, rejected: requestEnded by outcome, per replica where dispatched, and fleet;\n  - ttftSumMs and ttftCount: at first token, TTFT = firstTokenMs − arriveMs;\n  - e2eSumMs and e2eCount: at the end of finished requests;\n  - tpotSumMs and tpotCount: at the end, (endMs − firstTokenMs) ÷ (outputDone − 1), when outputDone ≥ 2;\n  - offered, organic, and retries (fleet): requestArrived, with attempt 0 counted as organic and attempt > 0 as a retry;\n  - readyReplicas (fleet level): tracked from replicaState.\n- **From meters:** diffs of every ReplicaMeters counter; level means via `takeLevelMean` for kvUsed (kvUsedFrac), running, waiting, and outstanding; kvUsedFracMax from the level's max, read before the take; and the abandonedSessions diff.\n- **Fleet aggregation, per the results.ts comment:** sums add across replicas; the 'mean' levels are summed across replicas, except kvUsedFrac, which is the mean across Ready replicas.\n\n**Histograms, per series, one bucket per `histBucketMs`:** TTFT counted in the bucket of its first-token time; E2E and TPOT in the bucket of the end time. Record against the serving replica and the fleet.\n\n**Records:**\n- RequestBlock with scope `all` when `input.detail === 'all'`; otherwise `tracked`, holding only `input.trackedAnalyst`'s requests. Each record is written at requestEnded from the request table slot, which is still readable then.\n- TransitionBlock rows from requestState notices, filtered by the same scope.\n- replicaEvents from replicaState.\n- Use the stable request `id`, not the slot.\n\n**Chunks.** `produceChunk(span)` returns the complete buckets and the records produced in [span.from, span.to). A bucket is complete when its end ≤ span.to. Advancing in many small steps must yield exactly the same data as one big step once concatenated.\n\n**Rollup** (01 §8), per replica for the day: requestsServed, meanE2eMs, meanNvidiaSmiUtil, and deliveredAtMs via `rollupDeliveryMs`. meanNvidiaSmiUtil is busyMs over the shift window (config.shift); document that choice.\n\n**Invariants:** accumulator arrays are sized for the replicas; no record is emitted twice; bucket starts are aligned.\n\n## Tests\nUse a stub module that allocates requests from events and emits the lifecycle topics with chosen times, plus hand-set meters and levels.\n\nCover:\n- each scalar and its fleet aggregation;\n- histogram counts and quantiles against the known samples;\n- tracked vs. all scope;\n- transition rows;\n- replica events;\n- chunk splitting: many small advances equal one big advance, including buckets exactly on boundaries;\n- checkpoint and restore mid-bucket;\n- rollup math;\n- slice size stays bounded over a long synthetic day. Report the peak slice size and structuredClone time in the handoff.\n\n## Handoff note (your final reply)\n- Branch and worktree path.\n- The API.\n- Choices, such as the utilization denominator and edge cases.\n- Test counts.\n- Contract-change requests.\n- Surprises."

Contracts I added or tightened: - How engine modules talk to each other. Engine modules now share one table of live requests and pass requests along through six messages: arrived, dispatched, first token, ended, cancelled, state changed. Ownership of every field and counter is documented. This let four engine packages start at once instead of one after another. - Protocol fixes from U2 and U8. Older in-flight results are dropped only where a fork actually replaces them. The engine must also report requests already in progress at the start of each detail window, or they'd vanish from the canvas. - K27, same-instant ordering. A first token that lands exactly at the timeout counts as on time.

Branch: worktree-agent-afc9b14d40f27b421 Worktree: /home/user/yosyp-work/inference-simulator/.claude/worktrees/agent-afc9b14d40f27b421

The spike is done and the report is written. The branch has 2 commits, rebased on current main (d46d3a2), and pnpm verify passes. Nothing is pushed. I only touched spikes/scale/ and docs/spikes/s1-scale.md. There are no contract changes and no new dependencies.

The worktree was first created from the repo's first commit, not main, so I fast-forwarded it to main before starting. E1–E4 merged while I worked, and I rebased onto them.

Report: docs/spikes/s1-scale.md. It covers method, a numbers table, the §5 cost projection, recommendations, a verdict per budget, what the simplifications hide, and notes for other work packages.

### Headline finding The biggest engine cost at Server B scale is KV block bookkeeping, not engine steps. - Once the pool is warm, every block allocation evicts a cached block. That is about 51M allocate, evict, register and release cycles per busy day. - E4's merged block manager costs about 140 ns per cycle under plain Node. That is about 7 s per Server B day, as much as the whole spike engine. - A typed-array layout (per-block owner and index arrays, plus a per-session chain of cached blocks) measured 12 ns per cycle in the same harness. Event-jumping doesn't reduce this cost. - I recommend E4 get its cycle to 30 ns or less. Otherwise the latest Server B entry point moves from about 09:30 to about 09:00.

### Load and scale Measured on a Wednesday at knee load (8 replicas × 400 analysts, 16 sessions per analyst per day, 5 turns per session, round-robin routing): - Knee: about 1.3 requests/s per replica at the 10:30 peak, about half of 02 §5's estimate of 3. - Why: under round-robin every turn re-prefills its whole history (87% of processed tokens are prefill). So the knee is compute-bound, and the KV pool stays mostly full only past the knee. - Volume: 282k requests/day and 1.23M/week. The overload case (20 sessions per analyst per day) gives 1.53M/week. - Events per week: about 6M model events, 4M event-jumping segments, 50M engine steps and 250M block evictions. 02 §5 had estimated 10–15M events.

### Speed - The spike ticks every step. It runs a day in 7–8 s, about 3,000–5,000 simulated s per wall s. With a realistic prefix cache it runs at 2,200–3,900× at peak. - Model events run at about 190k/s. - Event-jumping is projected to save only about 2× in wall time. Steps between events are about 7 at peak and 13 over the day, and each jump still costs work per running request. - 05:00→10:00 (P1's cost for a 10:00 entry): 3.0–3.7 s ticking at full fidelity. Projected 1.5–2.7 s with event-jumping, plus 0.2–0.4 s startup. E4 as merged would add about 1.6 s.

### Memory - (a) Full records plus transitions for a week: 389 MB with 1-minute chunks, 324 MB with 5-minute, 315 MB with 15-minute. That is 154 bytes per request, or 154 MB per million requests. - (b) Tracked analyst only: 200 MB with 1-minute chunks, 134 MB with 5-minute. With the recommended layouts it's about 40 MB. - Per-chunk overhead: about 12 KB of JS objects per chunk, which is 84 MB/week at 1-minute chunks. - Scalars per week: 10 s buckets 50 MB (24 MB if only the active window is emitted); 30 s buckets 17 MB. - Histograms per week at 60 s, current bins: 66 MB dense Uint32, 33 MB Uint16, 4.9 MB sparse (CSR). Uint16 is safe: the largest count in a bin is 191. - Checkpoints: - 1.0 MB with no hashing, 1.5–1.8 MB with the typed-array prefix cache, 2.2 MB with a Map. - With E4's pools as merged, about 3 MB (309 KB per replica, including a 70 KB scratch array). - Clone time is 2–6 ms. Restores are bit-identical. - Replaying 15 simulated minutes at peak takes 0.28–0.35 s.

### Percentile error (TTFT p99, fleet × 1-minute windows) Median / p95 / max relative error: - Current bins: 2.2 / 7.9 / 14.1%. - Twice the bins: 1.3 / 4.5 / 7.0%.

The worst case is bounded by one bin width, so 02 §11's "about 1–2%" only holds as a median.

### Recommendations - Chunk size: 5 simulated minutes. Send a 1-minute chunk first after init or a fork, and one chunk for the whole night. - Checkpoints: every 15 simulated minutes, kept for the playhead's day. - Buckets: bucketMs 10 s and histBucketMs 60 s, emitted only over each day's active window. - Histograms: sparse (CSR) with twice the current bins (ttft 192, tpot 128, e2e 192; bin ratio about 1.075), about 7 MB/week. The fallback is dense Uint16. Either way this changes the HistogramBlock contract, so the integrator must approve it. - Per-request storage: detail on demand for Server A and B. Keep full records for 1 GPU and 2 replicas. - Latest Server B entry for P1: 09:30 is safe and 10:00 is likely, given a fast KV layout; about 09:00 with E4 as merged. Server A can enter around 12:00; 1–2 replicas can enter any time.

### Budgets - P1 (≤ 3 s): pass for an entry at or before 09:30 if E4 gets faster; only about 09:00 as merged. - P2 (≤ 1 s): pass, projected. - P3 (≥ 1,000× at peak): pass. Projected 3,000–6,600× with a fast KV layout, 1,700–2,500× with E4 as merged. - P4 (≤ 400 MB): pass with detail on demand, about 200–280 MB projected (about 260–340 MB with E4's pools as merged). Fail with full records, about 470–620 MB. - P5 (no long tasks): uncertain; can't be measured in Node. - P6 (bundle size): not affected.

### What's uncertain - Event-jumping: the ~2× saving is estimated from step counts and a profile, not measured. - Laptop speed: unknown. Treat projections as ±50%. - Browser baseline: P4 assumes 100–150 MB for the React, canvas and SVG page; that isn't measured. - Noise: this is a shared host, and 30-minute windows varied ±30% between runs, so I report ranges. - Not modelled: retries, admission control, crashes, routing policies other than round-robin, and router overhead. The prefix cache was off in the headline runs (only its cost was measured). - Workload and calibration: the workload parameters are placeholders and the calibration is provisional, so the knee will move. - Merged modules: E3's step-cost formula matches the spike's. E4 was measured on its own (spikes/scale/kv-cycle.ts), not run inside the spike engine.

### For the author to decide - Block allocation (E5): 02 §7 rule 1 allocates blocks for the whole uncached prompt at admission, while vLLM V1 allocates per scheduled chunk. They differ for prompts longer than 8,192 tokens, which matters for tab 1. - 02 §5 and §11 estimates: should be updated with the measured figures above.

## 1. Summary

A deliberately simple Server B engine (8 replicas, knee-level load, ticking every engine step) runs a busy Wednesday in 7–8 s of wall time on this host. That is about 3,000–5,000 simulated seconds per wall second, and 2,200–3,900× once a realistic prefix cache is added. A knee-level week is 1.2–1.5M requests. Full per-request records plus transitions cost 154 bytes per request, so the week's records alone take 190–235 MB.

**The biggest engine risk is KV block bookkeeping, not steps.** Once the pool is warm, every block allocation evicts a cached block: about 51M allocate, evict, register and release cycles per knee day. E4's merged block manager costs about 140 ns per cycle under plain Node, which is about 7 s per Server B day, as much as the whole spike engine. An owner-array layout measured 12 ns per cycle in the same harness (§5, §8). Event-jumping does not reduce this cost.

| Budget | Verdict | One line |
|---|---|---|
| P1 first frame ≤ 3 s | **Pass for entry ≤ 09:30 if E4's per-block cost drops to about 30 ns; with E4 as merged, only about 09:00** (Server B, knee load) | 05:00→10:00 takes 3.0–3.7 s ticking at full fidelity; event-jumping should cut that to 1.5–2.7 s plus 0.2–0.4 s to start. E4 as merged would add about 1.6 s by 10:00. |
| P2 fork ≤ 1 s | **Pass** (projected) | Restore 2–6 ms, then replay ≤ 15 sim-min at peak (0.28–0.35 s ticking) and one short chunk |
| P3 ≥ 1,000× at peak | **Pass** | 2,200–3,900× ticking with a prefix cache; projected 3,000–6,600× with event-jumping, or 1,700–2,500× with E4 as merged |
| P4 ≤ 400 MB | **Pass with detail on demand; fail with full records** | The records store alone is 315–390 MB per knee week; tracked-only with the recommended layouts is about 40 MB |
| P5 no long tasks | **Uncertain** | Not measurable in Node. A chunk transfer costs 0.14 ms, so the risk is main-thread indexing and rendering |
| P6 JS ≤ 300 KB | **Not affected** | The spike adds nothing to the bundle |

Recommendations, in one table (reasons in §6):

| Item | Recommendation |
|---|---|
| Chunk size | 5 simulated minutes. After init or a fork, send a 1-minute chunk first. Send one chunk for the off-shift night, not 5-minute empties. |
| Checkpoint interval | 15 simulated minutes, kept for the playhead's day. Keep at most hourly checkpoints for other days, or none. |
| bucketMs / histBucketMs | 10 s / 60 s, emitted only over each day's active window (06:30–18:00 here) |
| Histograms | Sparse (CSR) storage with 2× the current bins (ttft 192, tpot 128, e2e 192; bin ratio ≈ 1.075). If dense storage stays, use Uint16. |
| Per-request storage | Detail on demand for Server A and B (the tracked analyst always has full records). Full records for 1 GPU and 2 replicas. |
| Latest Server B entry | 09:30 is safe and 10:00 is likely, given a fast KV layout. About 09:00 with E4 as merged. Server A can enter around 12:00 (projected); 1–2 replicas can enter at any time. |
| KV block manager (E4, merged) | Get the allocate, evict, register and release cycle from about 140 ns to 30 ns or less, for example with per-block owner arrays plus a per-session chain in place of `Map<owner, number[]>`. Keep scratch arrays out of checkpointed state. "Evict" is a counter, never a transition. |

/**
 * Percentiles per point from the merged histogram of its buckets (exact merge). counts is the
 * number of samples per point: 0 for computed but empty points, NaN where no bucket exists.
 */
export function quantileSeries(
  index: SlotIndex<HistogramBlock>,
  metric: HistogramMetric,
  series: number,
  window: TimeWindow,
  columns: number,
  quantiles: readonly number[],
): QuantileData {
  const bucketMs = index.bucketMs;
  const { t0, n, stepMs } = chooseGrid(window, columns, bucketMs || FALLBACK_STEP_MS);
  const t = new Float64Array(n);
  const counts = new Float64Array(n).fill(NaN);
  const values = quantiles.map(() => new Float64Array(n).fill(NaN));
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) t[i] = t0 + i * stepMs;
  if (bucketMs === 0) return { t, stepMs, values, counts };

  const spec = HISTOGRAM_SPECS[metric];
  const bins = spec.bins;
  const scratch = new Uint32Array(bins);
  const perPoint = stepMs / bucketMs;
  let present = 0;
  let first: Uint32Array = scratch;
  let firstOffset = 0;
  const visit = (block: HistogramBlock, bucket: number, count: number) => {
    if (series < 0 || series >= block.series) return;
    const data = block.data[metric];
    const stride = block.series * bins;
    let offset = (bucket * block.series + series) * bins;
    for (let k = 0; k < count; k++, offset += stride) {
      if (present === 0) {
        first = data;
        firstOffset = offset;
      } else {
        if (present === 1) for (let b = 0; b < bins; b++) scratch[b] = first[firstOffset + b]!;
        mergeInto(scratch, 0, data, offset, bins);
      }
      present++;
    }
  };
  let g = t0 / bucketMs;
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++, g += perPoint) {
    present = 0;
    forEachRun(index, g, g + perPoint, visit);
    if (present === 0) continue;
    // A single bucket is read in place; only merges use the scratch histogram.
    const hist = present === 1 ? first : scratch;
    const offset = present === 1 ? firstOffset : 0;
    counts[i] = totalCount(hist, offset, bins);
    for (let q = 0; q < quantiles.length; q++) {
      values[q]![i] = quantile(spec, hist, offset, quantiles[q]!);
    }
  }
  return { t, stepMs, values, counts };
}
----
function replicaSnapshots(state: StoreState, around: Around, t: SimMs): ReplicaSnapshot[] {
  const events = dayAt(state, t)?.events ?? [];
  const out: ReplicaSnapshot[] = [];
  for (let r = 0; r < state.replicas; r++) out.push(replicaSnapshot(state, around, events, r, t));
  return out;
}

function ttftP99At(hist: SlotIndex<HistogramBlock>, t: SimMs): number {
  if (hist.bucketMs === 0) return NaN;
  const g = Math.floor(t / hist.bucketMs);
  const e = entryAt(hist, g);
  if (!e) return NaN;
  const spec = HISTOGRAM_SPECS.ttft;
  const offset = (bucketIn(hist, e, g) * e.block.series + FLEET_SERIES) * spec.bins;
  return quantile(spec, e.block.data.ttft, offset, 0.99);
}

/**
 * Sparse histograms for `cells` cells (cell = bucket × series + seriesIndex). Cell i's nonzero bins
 * are bins[offsets[i] .. offsets[i+1]) with their counts, bins ascending. offsets has cells + 1 entries.
 */
export interface SparseHistograms {
  offsets: Uint32Array;
  bins: Uint16Array;
  counts: Uint32Array;
}

export function emptySparse(cells: number): SparseHistograms {
  return { offsets: new Uint32Array(cells + 1), bins: new Uint16Array(0), counts: new Uint32Array(0) };
}

/** Converts dense counts (cells × bins, cell-major) to sparse form. */
export function sparseFromDense(dense: Uint32Array, cells: number, bins: number): SparseHistograms {
  let nnz = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < cells * bins; i++) if (dense[i]! !== 0) nnz++;
  const out: SparseHistograms = {
    offsets: new Uint32Array(cells + 1),
    bins: new Uint16Array(nnz),
    counts: new Uint32Array(nnz),
  };
  let k = 0;
  for (let c = 0; c < cells; c++) {
    out.offsets[c] = k;
    const base = c * bins;
    for (let b = 0; b < bins; b++) {
      const n = dense[base + b]!;
      if (n !== 0) {
        out.bins[k] = b;
        out.counts[k] = n;
        k++;
      }
    }
  }
  out.offsets[cells] = k;
  return out;
}

/** Adds one sparse cell's counts into a dense histogram at target[targetOffset .. + bins). */
export function addSparseCellInto(
  target: Uint32Array,
  targetOffset: number,
  sparse: SparseHistograms,
  cell: number,
): void {
  const end = sparse.offsets[cell + 1]!;
  for (let k = sparse.offsets[cell]!; k < end; k++) {
    target[targetOffset + sparse.bins[k]!]! += sparse.counts[k]!;
  }
}

export function sparseCellCount(sparse: SparseHistograms, cell: number): number {
  let n = 0;
  const end = sparse.offsets[cell + 1]!;
  for (let k = sparse.offsets[cell]!; k < end; k++) n += sparse.counts[k]!;
  return n;
}
"""
open(p,'w').write(s)

function directHistogram(metric: HistogramMetric, series: number, t: number, step: number) {
  const bins = HISTOGRAM_SPECS[metric].bins;
  const merged = new Uint32Array(bins);
  let buckets = 0;
  for (const c of chunks) {
    const h = c.histograms;
    for (let i = 0; i < h.count; i++) {
      const start = h.startMs + i * h.bucketMs;
      if (start < t || start >= t + step) continue;
      buckets++;
      const offset = (i * h.series + series) * bins;
      for (let k = 0; k < bins; k++) merged[k]! += h.data[metric][offset + k]!;
    }
  }
  return { merged, buckets };
}

### Exported API (src/ui/timeline/index.ts) - WeekTimeline({ store }: WeekTimelineProps): goes in the AppShell timeline slot. It is 72 px tall; with the shell's top border the slot is 73 px, which matches "plus borders" in the layout doc. - TIMELINE_VIEW_HZ = 4: re-render rate for markers, computed ranges and mode. - TIMELINE_CLOCK_HZ = 8: re-render rate for the readout and aria-valuetext. These only change once per simulated minute. - loadedScenario(store): returns the store's .scenario, or null. - formatDayTime(t): returns text like "Wednesday 10:32". U6 can use it too.

### What it shows and does - Layout: - a readout column with the day and time, and "Computing…" in a role="status" region while buffering; - five day labels, with the current day in bold; - off-shift hours shaded from sim.shift, plus midnight dividers; - a computed strip under the bar that handles days finishing out of order; - rollup-arrival ticks at 12:00 the next day, in High-side mode only. - Markers: neutral ink plus a glyph (K26): - the lesson moment is a yellow triangle with an ink edge, and a thin yellow band through the bar; - each fork is a dashed line plus a flag.

Each marker is a button that seeks to its time. Its label shows on hover or focus.
  • Playhead: a separate element whose position is updated every frame through subscribeFrame, so the bar itself doesn't re-render as it plays. A test checks the playhead moves on each of 60 frames at 1000× while React commits at most 10 times. While buffering, the knob is hollow and pulses; the pulse is turned off under reduced motion.
  • Keyboard: the bar is a role="slider" with aria-valuetext like "Wednesday 10:28" and an aria-describedby summary of what's computed.
    • Arrow keys step 1 minute; Shift+Arrow steps 1 hour.
    • PageUp and PageDown step a day and keep the time of day.
    • Home goes to Monday's shift start, End to Friday's shift end, matching aria-valuemin and aria-valuemax.
  • Pointer: click and drag seek, with pointer capture. Scrubbing or pressing keys while playing keeps playing, because both go through store.seek.
  • Before a scenario loads: the slider is aria-disabled and ignores input.

### Tests 51 in src/ui/timeline: - geometry.test.ts (19): the time and position maths, snapping, stepping and text formatting. - WeekTimeline.test.tsx (32): rendering from store state, pointer seeks, every key binding with its aria-valuetext, the playhead following playback, and rendering with zero computed ranges or no scenario. It uses createFixturePlaybackStore with the manual clock and a task queue.

The full suite is 650 passed and 3 skipped; the skips were already there.

I also checked it in Chromium, since jsdom has no layout. At 1280 and 1440 wide the day labels fit without truncating or overlapping, and there were no console errors. The preview page I used for this is deleted and was never committed.

### What X1 needs - Pass the store from createPlaybackStore or createFixturePlaybackStore (U6 places the component). The shift and lesson marker come from that store's .scenario. With any other store, the timeline treats the whole day as on-shift and shows no lesson marker. - Switching from the fixtures to the worker doesn't affect the timeline. - For budget P5, confirm in Chrome that the playhead is smooth at 1000×. It writes style.left once per frame.

### Contract-change requests 1. Add readonly scenario: Scenario | null to PlaybackStore in src/playback/types.ts. Today only EngineClientStore has it, so the timeline has to cast. U6 will probably need the scenario too, for the trigger and sidebar. 2. Move a small test workaround into src/test-setup.ts. Tests that use user-event under Vitest's fake timers hang: Testing Library waits on a setTimeout(0) that it only advances under Jest's fake timers. The fix is to set globalThis.jest = { advanceTimersByTime: vi.advanceTimersByTime } while fake timers are on. It lives in my test file for now; the shared setup would save U3, U4 and U6 from hitting the same hang.

### Decisions for you or the author to confirm These probably deserve a row in the decision log: - Keyboard steps skip nights. They move in shift time, so ArrowRight at Monday 16:59 lands on Tuesday 07:00, matching how playback skips nights (K16). - Pointer seeks into shaded hours snap to the nearer shift edge. - Clicking a marker seeks to the marker's own time, not to the tab's entry point. - The buffering copy is "Computing…".

### Surprises - Markers were hidden behind the playhead. Tabs open 2 minutes before the lesson moment, which is about 0.3 px on the bar, and forks are created at the playhead. With a handle on top of the bar, the lesson marker was covered at every tab entry, and a fork from the trigger was covered too. The fix: - the lesson triangle sits above the bar; - fork flags sit inside the bar's top edge; - the playhead handle is a knob on the computed strip below.

This only showed up in the Chromium screenshots; jsdom can't catch it.
  • Other incident markers aren't drawn. 05 §5 mentions incident markers generally, and events such as crashes in baselinePatches could supply them. The U5 scope only asked for the lesson moment and forks; on tabs 5 and 6 the lesson moment is the incident.
  • main moved twice during the work (U8 and S1, then E7). I rebased; none of the files the timeline depends on changed.

          // A mouse click focuses too; a tooltip opened by it would sit over neighbouring controls
          // and swallow the next click. Show on focus only when no pointer press caused it.

          else show('focus');

          pressed.current = true;
          clearTimer();
          setHovered(false);
          setFocused(false);
          setDismissed(true);
        }}"""

open(p,'w').write(s)

function Toolbar({ delayMs = 50 }: { delayMs?: number }) {
  return (
    <>
      <Button>Before</Button>
      <Tooltip content="Re-applies the lesson moment at the playhead" delayMs={delayMs}>
        <Button aria-describedby="hint">Trigger</Button>
      </Tooltip>
      <p id="hint">Hint</p>
    </>
  );
}

const trigger = () => screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Trigger' });

E5 is done. pnpm verify passes on the branch after merging current main (E7, E9 and K29): 712 tests pass, 5 skipped. There is one change request for E7 (item 1 below); nothing else blocks the merge.

  • Branch: worktree-agent-ac7772e5c1fd09a4e

  • Edits: only under src/engine/replica/. No contract files touched.

### API - replicaModule: jumping on. This is what E11 wires in, in the documented module order. - createReplicaModule({ eventJumping?, onStep? }): - eventJumping: false runs one event per step through the same code. This is the cross-check E10 and my tests use. - onStep(StepInfo) is a trace hook: per step or span it reports clock, decode seqs/context, chunks [slot, prior, n]…, step count and duration. It is not stored in state. - Calls for other modules: - syncReplica(state, ctx, r) applies the lazy part of a span up to now. - replicaKvUsedFrac(state, ctx, r) and replicaGeneratedTokens(state, ctx, slot) sync first, then read. - replicaRunningCount, replicaWaitingCount and replicaStateCode are exact without a sync. - Other exports: EV_STEP_END = 300 (priority engine), EV_KICK = 301 (priority late), MODE, PHASE, engineLimits, and the slice types. - For E9: - Notices follow the new timing rules. firstToken and requestEnded go out from event handlers at firstTokenMs and endMs (= ctx.nowMs); onBucketEnd only applies lazy span state and pro-rates the meters. - All intermediate requestState notices are sent. I also send terminal ones; E9 ignores those. - I own every ReplicaMeters counter plus the kvUsed, running and waiting levels. They are exact at each bucket boundary, split per step. - For E10: harness.ts has a driver module, runScript, testConfig and withEngine. workload.ts has randomWorkload(seed, cal), keyed under Source.oracleWorkload with a distinct first key. Both are pure engine code, not exported from index.ts. - Ordering E10 must copy exactly, because LRU order depends on it: - Step end: decode block registrations for group (clock+1) % blockSize (admission order), then decode finishes (admission order), then chunks in scheduling order (register, first token, then finish or start decoding). - Release happens after registration.

### Behaviour choices where vLLM was ambiguous or 02 §7 decides - Step composition: - Decodes go first, then running prefills in admission order, then admissions (02 §7 rule 2). vLLM walks running requests in admission order, so an earlier long prefill can starve later decodes there. - A failed block allocation in any phase preempts the most recently admitted running request. If that request is a decode already scheduled this step, it is removed from the batch and returns its budget token. - Once a request preempts itself, no more running requests are scheduled. Nothing is admitted in a step that preempted. - Admission: FIFO with head-of-line blocking, gated on canAcquire for the whole uncached prompt; only the first chunk is allocated. - Metrics conventions: - Prefix query/hit counters count every admission, including re-admission after preemption. - cachedTokens holds the hits of the admission that produced the first token. - decodeTokens counts decode-step tokens only; the token sampled by a prefill chunk is not included. - Recompute counts prefill below the most KV the request ever had computed. - Resume after preemption: the request prefills prompt + output so far, and its last chunk samples the next token. - Kick at priority late, not the engine band: when work reaches an idle replica, the first step is composed once the instant settles, so same-instant dispatches share it. Step ends are at priority engine. - Cancel: removes the request at once and registers then releases its blocks. The step in flight keeps its cost; its meters include the cancelled request. - Leaving Ready: - Any move from Ready to another state fails everything held and wipes the pool. - Ready after that uses a fresh createKvPool. - Dispatch to a replica that isn't Ready fails at once. - Requests that could never run fail at dispatch with OUTCOME.failed. These are prompt + output > maxModelLen, or a KV need beyond the whole pool. Otherwise they would block the queue forever. - Config check: maxNumBatchedTokens < maxNumSeqs throws at init, as vLLM does. - Span precondition: a span only starts if the waiting head shares no session with a running request. Inside a span nothing can become admissible, except when the head's own session registers blocks, so this check makes the rule hold without assuming session coherence.

### Tests and throughput - 30 tests pass, 1 benchmark skipped unless E5_BENCH=1. All run with assertEveryEvent. - scheduler.test.ts (13): each rule, batch-1 TTFT and finish times against E3's reference helpers (including 3 chunks and system-prompt hits), first token exactly at the deadline, prefix reuse across turns, cold after eviction. - lifecycle.test.ts (7): cancel while waiting, in prefill, in decode, while preempted, and after the end; crash and rejoin; requests that can never run. - jumping.test.ts (8): 100 seeds comparing jumping to per-step, meters at every bucket boundary, checkpoint and restore mid-span. - integration.test.ts (2): with the real E7 router and E9 metrics. - Jumping vs per-step over 100 seeds (~3,400 requests): - Outcomes, preemptions, cached tokens, output done and state sequences are identical; TTFT and E2E agree to 1e-6 relative. - It covers about 100 preempted requests, over 2,800 cache hits, timeouts, crashes and more than 4,000 spans. - Meters agree to 1e-7 at every bucket boundary. - Throughput, one replica at the knee (0.55 sessions/s ≈ 2.1 req/s, busy 81%, TTFT p50 240 ms, p99 2.8 s):

Runner Mode Simulated s per wall s Events per wall s Events per sim hour
Plain Node (tsx) Jumping ~19,000 ~86,000 25k

Vitest Per step ~6,300 ~160,000 143k 
- Jumping cuts events 5.7× but wall time only about 2×, because KV block bookkeeping dominates. This matches S1's K28 finding.
- At about 19k simulated s per wall s per replica, 8 replicas leave headroom for P3's 1,000.

### Change requests 1. E7 (contract/API): refreshSignals reads meters.replica.kvUsed[r].value directly. Inside a span that value lags until the next event on that replica. Under kvUtilization routing, 4 of 20 two-replica seeds diverged between jumping and per-step runs. Calling syncReplica(state, ctx, r) before each read fixed all 20; I checked this with a stub module, without editing E7. refreshSignals needs ctx passed in for this. E10's two-replica oracle needs the fix. 2. Docs: a decision-log row for the choices above: decodes before running prefills, the kick at late, prefix counters on re-admission, and failing too-long requests. Also consider an OUTCOME code distinct from failed for "request too long"; for now it reuses failed, and E6 should cap history below maxModelLen.

### Surprises - A lazy-state ordering bug in onDispatched skewed the kvUsed bucket mean. The per-bucket meter test caught it, and it is fixed. An out-of-order KV level update inside a span now throws. - The knee under provisional calibration with multi-turn history (0.55–0.6 sessions/s, about 2.1 req/s) is lower than the old 3 req/s estimate. This agrees with K28.

Assistant2026-09-23T15:44:06.659Z

E5, the replica scheduler, is merged. It was the critical path, and all the engine modules now exist: RNG, event core, cost model, KV cache, scheduler, load generator, router and metrics.

What E5 does. It mirrors vLLM V1's batching: - decodes first, then chunked prefill; - admission only when the whole prompt fits; - when memory runs out, it preempts the most recently admitted request and recomputes it later; - prefix caching across turns.

Event-jumping and a step-by-step run give identical results across 100 random workloads, including preemptions, crashes and timeouts. One replica at the knee simulates about 19,000× faster than real time, well above the 1,000× target. Its detailed choices are recorded as K32.

A bug it surfaced in the router. The router sampled a replica's KV usage mid-computation, before the replica had brought that value up to date, so KV-based routing could differ between event-jumping and step-by-step runs. I added a small "sync before reading" message so the router always sees exact values.

Running now (6 agents): - E11, engine assembly and worker host. It connects the engine to the app, streams results and handles forks, and runs pnpm perf for the P1–P4 budgets. After it lands, X1 can switch the app from fixture data to the real simulation. - E10, the differential oracle. A second, deliberately simple simulator that must agree with the fast engine, including the router fix above. - E8, failure and recovery. - U4, charts. - E4b, KV speed-up.

### Exported API (src/charts/index.ts) - ChartStack({ store, chart3?, shift?, lessonMoment?, rollupRows?, calibration?, loadMetric?, width?, height?, maxHz? }) - chart3, shift and lessonMoment default to store.scenario, which landed on main during this WP. X1 can just mount <ChartStack store={store} />. - Width is measured with a ResizeObserver. Height defaults to 3 × 132 = 396 px: a 24 px zoom row, three 118 px charts, and an 18 px shared time axis. - It reads playheadMs, mode, forks, runId and index.version through usePlaybackSelector (10 Hz by default). - High-side pieces: - DailyBars({ rows, metric: 'meanE2eMs' | 'meanNvidiaSmiUtil' | 'requestsServed', replicas, width, height, title?, pendingDays?, progress? }), plus layoutBars. - NotCollectedPanel({ message?, title?, height? }), plus NOT_COLLECTED_MESSAGE. - pendingDaysAt(playheadMs, rows). - Collapse: useCollapseProgress(target, reduced, durationMs?, timer?), collapsePhases, collapseDomain. - Lower level: LineChart, panel builders (buildLatencyPanel, buildMemoryPanel, buildChart3Panel, buildLivePanels), window helpers (resolveWindow, zoomIn, zoomOut, ZOOM_SPANS_MS), formatters, and the types.

### Window and zoom - Default: the playhead's shift day, e.g. Wed 07:00–17:00. It moves to the next day when the playhead does. - Zoom steps: 4 h, 2 h, 1 h, 30 min, 15 min, using the −/+ buttons, +/-/0 on the focused stack, or ctrl/⌘ + wheel. - Paging: a zoomed window is a page that always contains the playhead, clamped inside the shift. During playback it flips to the next page at the right edge rather than sliding every frame. - Where zoom centres: buttons and keys keep the playhead at the same pixel. The wheel keeps the pointer's time in place, unless that would push the playhead out of view. - Reset: the "Shift day" button resets, and so does a new run (a tab load or Reset). - Queries: columns is the plot's pixel width. - Charts never show time after the playhead. Buckets draw once complete. Panels rebuild at most once per pixel of playhead movement. - Hover: hovering any chart draws a cursor across all three, with a time label on the axis. The legend doubles as the readout: values at the cursor, or the latest values when there is no cursor. - Keyboard: Left/Right, Home, End and Escape move the cursor on the focused stack, and an aria-live region announces the values.

### Thresholds and chart rules - Sparse buckets (chart 1): thresholds are P99_MIN_COUNT = 50 and MIN_LINE_POINTS = 12. - If the median non-empty point holds fewer than 50 requests, the p99 points are widened until a typical point holds 50. - If that leaves fewer than 12 points, the chart plots individual requests from requestPoints: TTFT as dots, E2E as rings, the slowest per pixel column. - The decision uses the whole window, so the chart doesn't flip modes while it plays. - Worst replica: - Latency: the highest p99 over the visible part of the window, from a single merged-histogram query per replica. - KV % and load: the highest mean over the visible part. - Other replicas are drawn as muted context lines; the worst gets a direct label such as "R3, worst". - Load fleet line: fleet outstanding ÷ readyReplicas. The loadMetric prop switches to running + waiting. - Paths: at most one vertex per pixel column, keeping the highest value.

### What X1 and U7 need - X1: mount <ChartStack store={store} /> in the AppShell charts slot. Then measure render cost at 1000× on Server B. Without it, ChartStack uses rows with deliveredAtMs ≤ playhead, and pendingDaysAt marks today and any undelivered earlier day. - High-side chart 3 shows daily bars only for utilization; the other kinds become "not collected" panels. That is my reading of 05 §9; please confirm.

### Contract-change requests None required. Decision-log rows I'd suggest: - Chart 1 uses a log y axis. E2E p99 sits 10–100× above TTFT, so on a linear axis it would flatten the TTFT lines that K14 says should lead. - Charts hide everything after the playhead. - The sparse thresholds above. - The High-side chart 3 reading above.

### Surprises - PlaybackStore.scenario landed mid-WP, which broke my test double; it's fixed, and I used it for the defaults. - The fake index's request counts are high (about 180/min per replica), so fixture tab 1 draws lines. Tests scale the counts down to exercise sparse mode. - S1's measured knee is about 1.3 requests/s per replica, around 78 per 60 s point. So point widening will kick in on 1-GPU tabs below the knee. - React registers wheel listeners as passive, so ctrl + wheel uses a native non-passive listener. - src/charts/test-support.ts is a non-test module used only by tests, the same pattern U8 uses.

  return createFixturePlaybackStore();
}

export function App(props: AppProps) {
  const scenarios = props.scenarios ?? defaultScenarios();
  const store = props.store ?? defaultStore();
  const calibration = props.calibration ?? appCalibration;
  const run = useScenarioRun(store, scenarios);
  const mode = usePlaybackSelector(store, selectMode);
  const [introOpen, setIntroOpen] = useState(true);

  return (
    <>
      <AppShell
        mode={mode}
        tabs={
          <ScenarioTabs
            scenarios={run.scenarios}

            onSelect={run.select}

Assistant2026-09-23T15:49:59.872Z

U4 (charts) is merged, and the app is now assembled end to end on fixture data. Verify passes with 955 tests, and the production-CSP smoke test passes.

  • Live mode:
  • The canvas shows eight replica lanes with KV tanks and request dots (queued, prefill, decode, preempted), and draws the tracked analyst's path with a "moved" marker when a turn lands on another replica.
  • Three aligned charts: latency (TTFT p99 and mean, E2E p99, worst replica), KV cache with preemption markers, and per-replica load. They never show time past the playhead.
  • The week timeline, the sidebar with a live status line, and the provisional-calibration footnote are all in place.
  • High-side mode:
  • The canvas goes quiet.
  • The charts collapse to daily bars, and KV and load read "Not collected on the high side".
  • At Wednesday 10:28 only Monday's rollup has arrived; Tuesday's shows "Arrives Wed 12:00". That's the delayed-telemetry lesson working.

Screenshots are in the session scratchpad as app-tab5-live.png and app-tab5-highside.png.

All sidebar and trigger copy is still placeholder text marked TODO(copy), until the scenario packages (C2/C3) write the real copy. The numbers are synthetic until the real engine is connected.

Running now (6 agents): - E11: engine assembly and worker, plus the pnpm perf budget numbers. - E10: the differential oracle. - E8: failure and recovery. - E4b: KV speed-up. - U7: High-side rollup table and a cross-surface consistency test.

Next: once E11 lands, the rest of X1 connects the app to the real simulation, and the scenario packages start. C1 builds the scenario runner, C2 writes tabs 1–3 with their lesson tests, then C3 does tabs 4–6.
